. The numerical convergence of TEBD with respect to
dt
is verified in Fig.
10.2
(a), where
dt
is gradually increased, and the same TEBD simulation as in Fig.
6.2
(b) is plotted.
In Fig.
10.2
(b), we focus on several time points in Fig.
10.2
(a) where the deviation compared to the
dt=10^{-3}J^{-1}
simulation becomes obvious. We see that these deviations in general reduce to
10^{-3}\sim 10^{-4}
at
dt=10^{-3}J^{-1}
, indicating that the TEBD Trotterisation error can be ignored in Fig.
6.2
(where this Trotterisation error is by orders of magnitude smaller than the deviation between TEBD simulation and the HFE stroboscopic Floquet Hamiltonian simulations).

Figure 10.2
:
(a) The TEBD simulation of return rate, Eq. (
6.13
) for
L=10
,
{\omega}=16J
,
U=8J
and
g={\omega}/4
. Noticeable deviations appear only when the Trotterisation step size
dt\gtrsim 0.004J^{-1}
.
(b) The deviation of (a) compared to the
dt=10^{-3}J^{-1}
TEBD simulation in Fig.
6.2
(b) at several simulation time points.

10.3.3
The accuracy of FSWT Hamiltonian at low frequencies

Here in Fig.
10.3
we show the return rate dynamics in Fig.
6.2
(c) for
\omega=8.5J
, where the FSWT stroboscopic dynamics (which ignores the
\hat{y}_{3}
correction) starts to noticeably deviate from the exact dynamics, showing a relative error
\mathcal{E}\sim 0.2
according to Eq. (
6.14
).

In addition, in Fig.
6.2
(c), the relative error
\mathcal{E}
of FSWT stroboscopic dynamics shows several local maxima around
{\omega}\approx 7J,7.5J,8J
and
9J
. Since the Mott gap is
U=3J
for the Hubbard chain simulated in Fig.
6.2
(c), these local maxima correspond to driving resonance above the Mott gap. Similar above-Mott-gap driving resonance has been reported previously in the linear absorption spectrum of the driven Hubbard clusters
[
136
]
. For the parameters considered in Fig.
6.2
(c), these resonances do not significantly affect the accuracy of our FSWT Hamiltonian (
6.12
).

Figure 10.3
:
The return rate Eq. (
6.13
) for
\omega=8.5J
,
U=3J
and
g={\omega}/4
. Noticeable deviations of FSWT dynamics appear at, e.g., time = 51
J^{-1}
.

10.4
Evaluation of the projection Eq. (
6.15
)

In the derivation of Eq. (
6.15
), we need to evaluate the projected Hamiltonian
\mathcal{P}\hat{h}\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}\equiv\Pi_{j=1}^{N}(1-\hat{n}_{j,\uparrow}\hat{n}_{j,\downarrow})
is the projector on the zero-doublon manifold. According to the definition of projector
\mathcal{P}
, we have

\begin{split}&\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}\mathcal{P}=\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}(1-\hat{n}_{j+1,s}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}})\mathcal{P}=0,\end{split}

(10.28)

and similarly
\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}\mathcal{P}=0
. Furthermore, since we consider the half-filling case, we have
\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\to 1-\hat{n}_{j,s}
, which results in

\begin{split}\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\mathcal{P}&\to\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}(1-\hat{n}_{j,s})\mathcal{P}\\
&=\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\mathcal{P}.\end{split}

(10.29)

Thus, when the projector
\mathcal{P}
is applied to the Floquet Hamiltonian (
6.12
), correct to
\mathcal{O}(J)
, we have

\begin{split}\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}\mathcal{P}&=\frac{g^{2}J}{{\omega}^{2}}\sum\limits_{s}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{L-1}(\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}+\hat{c}_{j+1,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j,s})\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}(\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}+\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}})+\delta^{\prime}\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}\right)\mathcal{P}\\
&=\frac{g^{2}J}{{\omega}^{2}}\sum\limits_{s}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{L-1}\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}(\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}+\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}})+\delta^{\prime}\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}\right)\mathcal{P}\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\hat{c}_{j+1,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j,s}\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}(\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}+\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}})+\delta^{\prime}\hat{n}_{j,\bar{s}}\hat{n}_{j+1,\bar{s}}\right)\mathcal{P}\\
&=\frac{g^{2}J}{{\omega}^{2}}\sum\limits_{s}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{L-1}(\hat{c}_{j,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j+1,s}+\hat{c}_{j+1,s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{j,s})\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}\right)\mathcal{P}\\
&=-\frac{g^{2}}{{\omega}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}\right)~~\times~~\hat{h}\mathcal{P},\\
\end{split}

(10.30)

and therefore

\begin{split}&\mathcal{P}(\hat{h}+\hat{H}^{\prime(2)})\frac{1}{-U}(\hat{h}+\hat{H}^{\prime(2)})\mathcal{P}\\
&\approx\frac{1}{-U}\mathcal{P}\hat{h}^{2}\mathcal{P}+\frac{1}{-U}\mathcal{P}\hat{h}\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}\mathcal{P}+\frac{1}{-U}\mathcal{P}\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}\hat{h}\mathcal{P}\\
&\approx\frac{1}{-U}\mathcal{P}\hat{h}^{2}\mathcal{P}\times\left(1-2\frac{g^{2}}{{\omega}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}\right)\right)\\
&=\bigg(\frac{4J^{2}}{U}(1-2\frac{g^{2}}{{\omega}^{2}})+4\frac{g^{2}J^{2}}{{\omega}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{U-{\omega}}+\frac{1}{{\omega}+U}\right)\bigg)\times\sum\limits_{j=1}^{L-1}{\bf S}_{j}\cdot{\bf S}_{j+1}.\end{split}

(10.31)

10.5
FSWT in strong-driving frame

We start from the following driven Hamiltonian

\begin{split}\hat{H}_{t}^{\text{lab}}&=-J\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}r_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}+U\sum_{{\bf j}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\uparrow}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\downarrow}\\
&+\sum_{{\bf j},s}{\epsilon}_{\bf j}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},s}+g\sum_{{\bf j},s}(\phi_{\bf j}e^{i{\omega}t}+\phi_{\bf j}^{*}e^{-i{\omega}t})\hat{n}_{{\bf j},s}.\end{split}

(10.32)

The coefficient
r_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}
allows the hopping to be non-uniform. The function
\phi_{\bf j}
maps the position vector
\bf j
into a complex value according to the laser drive in length gauge. For example,
\phi_{\bf j}=(j_{x}+i~j_{y})/\sqrt{2}
describes the coupling to a circularly polarised laser, while
\phi_{\bf j}=j_{x}
describes a linearly polarised laser.

To obtain driving effects at higher orders of driving strength
g
, we will apply FSWT (constructed in Chapter
5
) in the strong-driving rotating frame. To work in this strong-driving frame, we apply the following PZW-type transform
[
251
,
252
,
253
]
,
|\psi\rangle_{t}=\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t}|\psi\rangle_{t}^{\text{lab}}
, where

\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t}=\exp\bigg( \frac{g}{\omL} \sum_{{\bf j},s} \hat{n}_{{\bf j}s} (\phi_{\bf j} e^{i \omL t} - \phi_{\bf j}^* e^{-i \omL t}) \bigg).

(10.33)

The resulting rotating-frame Hamiltonian, according to
\hat{H}_{t}=\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t}\hat{H}_{t}^{\text{lab}}(\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t})^{{\dagger}}+i(\partial_{t}\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t})(\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t})^{{\dagger}}
, reads

\begin{split}\hat{H}_{t}&=-J\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}e^{iA_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\sin(\omL t + B_{{\bf j},{\bf i}})}r_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\\
&+\sum_{{\bf j},s}{\epsilon}_{\bf j}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},s}+U\sum_{{\bf j}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\uparrow}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\downarrow}\end{split}

(10.34)

where
A_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv\frac{2g}{{\omega}}|\phi_{\bf j}-\phi_{\bf i}|
and
B_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv\arg(\phi_{\bf j}-\phi_{\bf i})
. This is a multi-frequency driving Hamiltonian whose
driving strength becomes the hopping parameter

J
, which can be written as

\begin{split}\hat{H}_{t}=\hat{H}^{(0)}+\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}\hat{H}^{(1)}_{j}e^{ij{\omega}t},\end{split}

(10.35)

where the superscript represents the orders of
J
. In Eq. (
10.35
), the undriven part no longer contains hopping, as given by

\hat{H}^{(0)}=\sum_{{\bf j},s}{\epsilon}_{\bf j}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},s}+U\sum_{{\bf j}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\uparrow}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\downarrow},

(10.36)

and the driving term oscillating at
e^{ij{\omega}t}
reads

\hat{H}^{(1)}_{j}=-J\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s},

(10.37)

where we define
\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv e^{ijB_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}\mathcal{J}_{j}(A_{{\bf j},{\bf i}})r_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}
, which satisfies
\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}=\left(\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}\right)^{*}
since the driven Hamiltonian is Hermite. Here
\mathcal{J}_{j}
is the
j
th kind Bessel function.
In this rotating frame, the driving term has a static component
\hat{H}^{(1)}_{0}
.

FSWT in this strong-driving frame finds a time-periodic unitary transform, i.e., the micro-motion operator
\hat{U}_{t}=\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}e^{ij{\omega}t}\hat{f}^{(k)}_{j}-H.c.\right)
, which eliminates the oscillation in
\hat{H}_{t}
perturbatively in orders of driving strength
J
. The resulting time-independent Floquet Hamiltonian
\hat{H}^{\prime}
gives the evolution operator
\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{t,t_{0}}
. The whole process decouples the lab-frame evolution operator, such that
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{t,t_{0}}^{\text{lab}}=(\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t})^{{\dagger}}\hat{U}_{t}^{{\dagger}}\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{t,t_{0}}\hat{U}_{t_{0}}\hat{U}^{\text{r}}_{t_{0}}
.

The
\mathcal{O}(J)
lowest order Sylvester equation is, for all
j\neq 0
,

\hat{H}^{(1)}_{j}+[\hat{f}^{(1)}_{j},\hat{H}^{(0)}]-j{\omega}\hat{f}^{(1)}_{j}=0,

(10.38)

whose solution is

\hat{f}^{(1)}_{j}=-J\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}\frac{\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}

(10.39)

where we have defined the parameter
{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv j{\omega}+{\epsilon}_{\bf j}-{\epsilon}_{\bf i}
and the operator

\hat{\theta}^{s,s^{\prime}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv 1+\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j}s}+\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{n}_{{\bf i}s^{\prime}}+\delta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j}s}\hat{n}_{{\bf i}s^{\prime}}

(10.40)

where the coefficients

3
3

3

For compactness, the dependence on the Fourier index
j
is abbreviated in the notation of
{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}},\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}},\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\text{ and }\delta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}
.

are defined as

\begin{split}\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}&=\frac{-U}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}+U}~~~~~\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}=\frac{U}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}-U}\\
\delta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}&=-\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}-\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}.\end{split}

(10.41)

then the Floquet Hamiltonian, correct to
\mathcal{O}(J^{2})
, reads

\begin{split}\hat{H}^{\prime}&=\hat{H}^{\prime(0)}+\hat{H}^{\prime(1)}+\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}+\mathcal{O}(J^{3})\\
&=\hat{H}^{(0)}+\hat{H}^{(1)}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left([\hat{f}^{(1)}_{j},\hat{H}^{(1)}_{-j}]+H.c.\right)\\
&=-J\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}\mathcal{J}_{0}(A_{{\bf j},{\bf i}})~r_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}+\sum_{{\bf j},s}{\epsilon}_{\bf j}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},s}+U\sum_{{\bf j}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\uparrow}\hat{n}_{{\bf j},\downarrow}\\
&~~~+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\left[\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle}\sum_{s}\frac{\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}},\sum\limits_{\langle{\bf i^{\prime}},{\bf j^{\prime}}\rangle}\sum_{s^{\prime}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j^{\prime}},{\bf i^{\prime}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j^{\prime}},s^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i^{\prime}},s^{\prime}}\right]~+~H.c.\right)\end{split}

(10.42)

In this result, the first line comes from
\hat{H}^{(0)}+\hat{H}^{(1)}_{0}
, where the hopping is renormalised by the dynamical localisation effect, with the intrinsic interactions in the undriven system unchanged. The second line contains the
\mathcal{O}(J^{2})
driving induced interaction, which evaluates to

\begin{split}\hat{H}^{\prime(2)}&=J^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j}\}}\sum_{s}(\hat{n}_{{\bf j}s}-\hat{n}_{{\bf i}s})\left(\frac{\big|\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\big|^{2}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}-\frac{\big|\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}\big|^{2}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}\right)\\
&+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j}\}}\sum_{s}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},{\bar{s}}}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},{\bar{s}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}~\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\bigg(\frac{\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}-\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}+\frac{\beta_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}-\gamma_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}\bigg)+H.c.\\
&+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j}\}}\sum_{s}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},{\bar{s}}}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},{\bar{s}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\bigg(-\frac{\big|\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\big|^{2}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}(\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}-\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}})-\frac{\big|\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}\big|^{2}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}(\beta_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}-\gamma_{{\bf i},{\bf j}})\bigg)+H.c.\\
&+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j-k}\}}\sum_{s}\hat{c}_{{\bf k},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\bigg(-\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\big(\frac{\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}+\frac{\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}\big)+\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\big(\frac{\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}+\frac{\hat{\theta}^{{\bar{s}},{\bar{s}}}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}\big)\bigg)+H.c.\\
&+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j-k}\}}\sum_{s}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf k},{\bar{s}}}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},{\bar{s}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\bigg(-\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\big(\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\bar{s}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}+\frac{\hat{\mu}^{s}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}\big)+\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\big(\frac{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}+\frac{\hat{\nu}^{\bar{s}}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}\big)\bigg)+H.c.\\
&+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\{{\bf i-j-k}\}}\sum_{s}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},s}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf j},{\bar{s}}}^{{\dagger}}\hat{c}_{{\bf k},{\bar{s}}}\hat{c}_{{\bf i},s}\bigg(\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}\big(\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\bar{s}}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}}-\frac{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}}\big)+\alpha^{[j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}\alpha^{[-j]}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\big(\frac{\hat{\mu}^{s}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf k}}}-\frac{\hat{\nu}^{\bar{s}}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}{{\omega}_{{\bf i},{\bf j}}}\big)\bigg)+H.c.\\
\end{split}

(10.43)

Unlike
\langle{\bf i},{\bf j}\rangle
which is nonequivalent to
\langle{\bf j},{\bf i}\rangle
, the summation
\{{\bf i-j}\}
here runs over all 2-site-connected bonds, thus
\{{\bf i-j}\}
and
\{{\bf j-i}\}
are equivalent which only need to be included once. Likewise, the summation
\{{\bf i-j-k}\}
runs over all 3-site-connected bonds where
\bf j
is the middle site, thus
\{{\bf i-j-k}\}
and
\{{\bf k-j-i}\}
are equivalent and should be included only once.
To shorten the forula, we have defined
\hat{\mu}^{s}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv\beta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}+\delta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{n}_{{\bf i}s}
and
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\equiv\gamma_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}+\delta_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\hat{n}_{{\bf j}s}
.

In the absence of the on-site potential
{\epsilon}_{\bf j}=0
, the above result is reduced according to
{\omega}_{{\bf j},{\bf i}}\to{\omega}
, and
\beta,\gamma,\delta
become spatial independent.
